## Sheet: Roadmap
| SponsorX — Full Programme Roadmap |  |  |  |  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Four blueprint phases, 354 tasks. Each phase has its own sheet. Every task says where the work physically happens. |  |  |  |  |  |  |
| Phase | Outcome | Tasks | Person-days | Blueprint timeline | Balanced budget | Depends on |
| Phase 1 | Managed micro-NIL marketplace — the §39 loop working for 25 athletes and 10 businesses | 249 | 601 | 14–18 weeks | $75K–$105K | Nothing — this is the start |
| Phase 2 | Transactional marketplace: external properties, payments, commission ledger, payouts, wallet rewards | 64 | 260 | 16–20 weeks | $80K–$120K | Phase 1 auth/RBAC, sponsor/property/inventory/campaign/reward models, Zoho integration, core analytics |
| Phase 3 | Dynamic pricing, sponsor matching, forecasting, attribution and the data platform | 44 | 184 | 18–22 weeks | $105K–$160K | Phase 2 transaction history, campaign performance data, marketplace activity, reliable event ingestion |
| Phase 4 | INFINEX virtual sponsorship inventory, activations, telemetry and cross-channel reporting | 51 | 207 | 18–22 weeks | $90K–$150K | Stable SponsorX APIs, campaign/reward engine, identity/session model, metrics pipeline |
| TOTAL | Full SponsorX platform |  |  | 66–82 weeks | $350K–$535K |  |
| WHERE THE WORK HAPPENS |  |  |  |  |  |  |
| Why this column exists | A task that says 'create the Zoho module' and a task that says 'author the Zoho mapping document' look identical in a list — but one is click-work in an admin UI and the other is a written spec. Every task now states which. |  |  |  |  |  |
| Document | A written decision or specification. No system is touched — the output is a document people agree on. |  |  |  |  |  |
| Zoho dashboard | Configuration clicked through the Zoho CRM admin UI (Setup → Modules and Fields, API credentials, users). |  |  |  |  |  |
| Vendor console | Configuration in a vendor's own UI — Railway, Cloudflare, Clerk, the payment or email provider. |  |  |  |  |  |
| Code | Work in the repository: schema, domain logic, routes, worker jobs, UI, tests, CI. |  |  |  |  |  |
| Code + console | Both: something configured at the vendor AND wired in the codebase. |  |  |  |  |  |
| Design tool | Visual design work producing artwork, diagrams or screen designs. |  |  |  |  |  |
| Document + code | A written policy that must then be enforced in the codebase. |  |  |  |  |  |
| External · counsel | Performed by a lawyer, not the team. Longest lead times in the programme — start these early. |  |  |  |  |  |
| HOW TO READ THIS WORKBOOK |  |  |  |  |  |  |
| One sheet per phase | Phase 1–4, identical 18 columns, so they behave the same way. |  |  |  |  |  |
| Task IDs | Phase 1: P{stage}-{CAT}-{nn}, e.g. P4-BE-03. Phases 2–4: {phase}S{sprint}-{CAT}-{nn}, e.g. 2S5-BE-04 = Phase 2, Sprint 5, Backend, task 04. IDs never change — cite them in branches and commits. |  |  |  |  |  |
| Order | A dependency-respecting sort key, recomputed whenever tasks are added. It can shift; the ID cannot. |  |  |  |  |  |
| Status | Phase 1 is live. Every Phase 2–4 task starts Blocked because Phase 1 must finish first. |  |  |  |  |  |
| Weight | S=1 · M=3 · L=5 person-days. Estimates for one person, not calendar commitments. |  |  |  |  |  |
| PHASE OVERLAP |  |  |  |  |  |  |
| Permitted | Begin Phase 3 warehouse and event modelling during the final 4–6 weeks of Phase 2. Begin the Phase 4 API contract and placement taxonomy during the final 4 weeks of Phase 3. |  |  |  |  |  |
| Forbidden | No ML pricing before clean history exists — launch Phase 3 on rules. No marketplace payouts before commission snapshots, reconciliation, refunds/disputes and audit logs are tested. No virtual impressions reported to sponsors before viewability and de-duplication are validated against pilot telemetry. |  |  |  |  |  |
| SCOPE REALITY |  |  |  |  |  |  |
| Team size | The blueprint staffs Phase 1 at 4.5–7.5 FTE and Phases 2–4 higher. The team is two people. Phase 1 alone is ~44 weeks at that size. Scope, not stack, is the binding constraint. |  |  |  |  |  |

Set a spend alert — usage-based hosting surprises people. | Done | 1 | 2026-09-18 | 2026-09-23 | rcfworks | 1 | P0-PMO-02 | Project exists in the confirmed region; billing and spend alerts configured | Guide §10 | Workspace 'rcarr-crypto's Projects'; project 'sponsorX' ID 1c11f29a-b569-4d14-98cf-e97a4d3ae209. PRO PLAN with spend limits set (compute hard $150 / email $25; agent hard $20 / email $20) - that half of the acceptance is met. REOPENED 2026-09-18: the region half is NOT met. Read from the Railway CLI, `region` is null on the service instance in BOTH environments - nothing was ever set explicitly. Railway therefore placed them by default: staging landed on US East, production landed on 'sfo' (San Francisco). The US East chip read in the console on 2026-09-18 was staging's. G-02 requires us-east4-eqdc4a, so production currently violates data residency. Fix is one serviceInstanceUpdate per environment setting region='us-east4-eqdc4a'; closes as soon as that is applied and must happen before Postgres exists under P2-OPS-01, because moving a database region later is exactly what G-02 was written to avoid. 2026-09-18 update: multiRegionConfig set to us-east4-eqdc4a on web in BOTH environments (the legacy `region` field is inert - Railway reads multiRegionConfig). web's running deployment still reports sfo and will move on its next redeploy, which this session is not permitted to trigger. CLOSED 2026-09-18: web's running deployment now reports multiRegionConfig {us-east4-eqdc4a: 1}. Both halves of the acceptance are met - region confirmed in the live deployment, billing and spend limits configured. 2026-09-18: set back to In progress under rcfworks at the user's instruction, so the whole Railway / Clerk / backend-setup cluster reads as one owned workstream. THE WORK ITSELF IS FINISHED and was completed on 2026-09-18 - the Date Done column still holds it. Status here signals ownership of the workstream, not that the work is outstanding. CLOSED 2026-09-23, verified in the console. PROJECT: sponsorX, environments production and staging; production runs web + Postgres-production. REGION: web is US East (Virginia, USA), matching A7's us-east4-eqdc4a. BILLING: Pro Workspace, card ending 1115, billing email rcarr@icarrefound.org. OWNERSHIP: the workspace reads 'rcarr-crypto's Project' — a personal workspace, confirmed by the user as the owner's own account and the one holding Pro; that is the BTG-controlled account for this purpose and the question is settled. SPEND ALERTS: compute hard limit $50 / email alert $25; agent hard limit $5 / alert $0 because nothing here uses Railway agents. Current usage $1.35 with $2.71 projected for the period against $20 included, so the limits are headroom against a runaway rather than a budget. |

| 7 | P0-DATA-01 | 0 | Foundation & Decisions | DATA | Document | Define the metric provenance taxonomy | Agree the five honesty labels for numbers: verified by API, verified by a human checking, self-reported by the athlete, estimated, or attributed. Write the rule for when each applies. | Done | 1 | 2026-09-14 | 2026-09-14 | rcfworks | 1 |  | The five labels (verified-API / verified-manual / self-reported / estimated / attributed) defined with a written rule for when each applies | §22, §26 | 2026-09-14 DRAFTED - documentation/SponsorX-Metric-Provenance-Taxonomy.md v0.1. Five labels defined with qualification rules and who may assign each. Adds the two rules the blueprint never gave: a DERIVED value takes the weakest of its inputs, and a ROLLUP takes the weakest of its parts; ATTRIBUTED propagates upward and sits off the strength ladder. Proposes splitting provenance into two fields - source (the five labels) and retrieval (the named path) - because the codebase already uses both conventions in one field. AUDIT FOUND 6 ISSUES, 3 high severity and 2 of those on the sponsor-facing report: F-1 campaign Total Views labelled VERIFIED_MANUAL but built from SELF_REPORTED athlete figures; F-2 Cost per View / per Engagement inherit the wrong provenance; F-3 'Primary Age' and 'Core Age' are audience demographics, out of scope for Phase 1 - remove, do not relabel. NOT DONE: 2 open decisions D1 (does VERIFIED_API cover our own instrumentation) and D2 (is the weakest-input rollup rule too harsh). No code changed. | FIXES APPLIED 2026-09-14 to src/lib/fixtures.ts: F-1 sponsor-report Total Views and Engagements VERIFIED_MANUAL -> SELF_REPORTED (rollups of athlete-reported figures); F-2 Cost per View and Cost per Engagement follow their weakest input; F-3 'Primary Age' and 'Core Age' REMOVED from property and athlete stats - demographics are out of Phase 1 scope with no retrieval path. Each edit carries a comment citing the rule. F-4/F-5/F-6 outstanding, none on the sponsor report. BUILD VERIFIED 2026-09-14: npm ci + npm run build clean (27 routes, 21 static pages), tsc --noEmit clean, lint 0 errors / 1 pre-existing warning in t/[code]/route.ts. | DONE 2026-09-14, both decisions taken. D1: a SIXTH label added - VERIFIED_SYSTEM for events SponsorX records itself (QR scan, redemption), displayed as 'measured', and it outranks VERIFIED_API because we can audit ours end to end. VERIFIED_API narrows to mean an outside platform and its name is finally accurate. Costs one Prisma enum value. D2: rollups SHOW THEIR MIX with percentages, falling back to the weakest label only where a single value must fit. Implemented: SourceLabel takes six labels; Leads Generated and Rewards Redeemed -> VERIFIED_SYSTEM (F-4). Build, typecheck and lint clean. STILL OPEN, neither on the sponsor report: F-5 property stats need stated evidence; F-6 homepage counters need the two-field source/retrieval change, which is a fixture SHAPE change and must land in the Prisma model too. |

R2 WAS NOT ENABLED AT ALL until today - this row had sat In progress since 2026-09-18 with empty notes, and the account's R2 page was still the activation screen. Worth knowing that an In progress row with no notes may mean 'not started'. Both buckets now exist, both pinned to EASTERN NORTH AMERICA (ENAM) by an explicit location hint. THE DEFAULT WOULD HAVE BEEN WRONG: Cloudflare's 'Automatic' placement offered ASIA PACIFIC on both creation screens, which would have put athlete agreements and minors' data outside the US-only residency decision (G-02) and a continent away from Railway's us-east4. Bucket location is PERMANENT, so this was a one-shot choice. Note R2's 'Specify jurisdiction' option does not help here - its only jurisdictions are eu and fedramp - so a location hint is the correct mechanism. SEPARATE POLICIES, which is the acceptance: sponsorx-private has Public Access DISABLED and no public hostname at all; sponsorx-public has the Public Development URL enabled at https://pub-7d82e60511bb4bdc9198b547f4e37fe9.r2.dev. VERIFIED FROM OUTSIDE THE ACCOUNT: the public host answers 404 from R2 itself (Server: cloudflare) for a missing object, proving it serves; the S3 API endpoint returns 400 for unsigned requests to BOTH buckets, proving that path always requires a signature. S3 API ENDPOINT for the backend's S3_ENDPOINT: https://0a10106029579f2496ccf0956745525c.r2.cloudflarestorage.com (the hex string is the Cloudflare account id; not a secret on its own). STILL TO DO, BELONGS ELSEWHERE: (1) the r2.dev URL is rate-limited and Cloudflare says explicitly it is not for production - a CUSTOM DOMAIN such as cdn.sponsorx.net should replace it before launch, and it is available today because sponsorx.net is in this same Cloudflare account. Seven artboards at 390px: offer, claim/consent, claimed, returning fan, already redeemed, expired, unavailable. ACCEPTANCE MET - consent language placed (above the button, below the field, four plain sentences, nothing pre-ticked), offer presentation designed, single-use and expired states designed. VERIFIED: every text/background pairing passes WCAG AA against its real background - lowest on-ground value is white at 50% = 5.36:1, the inverted code card is 18.4:1, the primary button 6.8:1; the four colours that fail against the dark ground are dark ink on the light card and are used correctly. No JavaScript in the design (the three script tags in the .dc.html are Claude Design canvas infrastructure, not page code); only an email field and a native required checkbox; no invented statistics, no countdown, no account or app prompt. THREE PROMISES THE COPY MAKES THAT NO TASK CURRENTLY DELIVERS, for decision before P6-FE-02 wires this page: (1) 'You can unsubscribe from any of it, at any time, in one tap' - zero tasks in the 348 mention unsubscribe or opt-out. THE BLOCKING IS STILL REAL - this row depends on P0-OPS-03, P2-BE-02. Read Depends On, not Status, to find what can actually be started. 2026-09-21 IMPLEMENTED, moved to Code review - the code is complete but one half of the 'Done when' is UNVERIFIED. Real sign-in works and is proven: all four portals and their nested pages 307 to /login when signed out, while /, /packages, /join, /map and the fan surfaces /r/[token] and /t/[code] stay open, as §16 requires. NOT proven: 'User.clerkId mirrors the Clerk identity' - the mirror needs a database and there is no local Postgres by design (Railway is private-networking only, no docker or psql on the machine), so it can only be exercised on a staging deploy. DESIGN NOTES THAT WILL OUTLIVE THIS ROW. (1) The file is src/proxy.ts, NOT middleware.ts: Next 16 deprecated the middleware file convention and renamed it, the exported function must be named proxy, and the edge runtime is unsupported under the new name. Clerk 7.9.2 already handles this - suggestMiddlewareLocation() looks for both names when Next is 16 or higher. (2) ROUTE PROTECTION IS NOT IN THE PROXY. Clerk deprecated createRouteMatcher() in this version with the reasoning that middleware path matching 'can diverge from how Next.js routes requests and leave protected resources reachable'. Protection therefore sits in each portal's layout.tsx via auth.protect(), which wraps every page beneath it so a new route cannot bypass it - and that is exactly where P2-BE-04's requireActor() belongs. The proxy now only attaches Clerk's request context. (3) NEXT_PUBLIC_CLERK_SIGN_IN_URL=/login is REQUIRED as an environment variable, not a ClerkProvider prop: Clerk's own note on auth() says server-side redirect URLs can only come from env vars. Without it, signed-out visitors are bounced to Clerk's hosted portal on accounts.dev instead of our designed screen. P2-OPS-04 must set it in Railway. (4) /portal is the post-sign-in router and the ONLY route that touches the database; every other screen still runs on fixtures, which is what keeps the app buildable and runnable with no DATABASE_URL. (5) THE MIRROR NEVER INVENTS A TENANT. A Clerk identity with no matching User row is authenticated but unprovisioned and is told so, because Phase 1 is a managed marketplace where access is granted by BTG staff rather than self-served. Linking is by verified email: an admin creates the User row ahead of time and first sign-in claims it by writing the real clerkId. (6) src/server/db.ts now builds the Prisma client on FIRST USE rather than on import - next build imports every route module to collect its config, so the eager client turned a missing DATABASE_URL into a build failure rather than a query failure. mock-auth.ts and login-form.tsx are deleted; the login screen keeps its two-panel design with Clerk's <SignIn routing='hash'> in the left panel. CLOSED 2026-09-21. The signed-in path was verified by the user on staging: /portal forwarded to /admin, which is only reachable if the mirror found the seeded row, wrote the real clerkId over the placeholder and read BTG_ADMIN back out of Postgres. All three clauses of the 'Done when' now hold - real sign-in works, User.clerkId mirrors the Clerk identity, and no tenant or role data is written to Clerk Organizations or session claims. 2026-09-21 PASSWORD RESET CONFIRMED WORKING on staging, end to end: Clerk emailed a code, accepted a new password and created a session, with no code of ours involved - <SignIn routing='hash'> drives the whole flow in the URL fragment. This also settles that the instance uses PASSWORDS rather than email codes. One thing seen and diagnosed: after the reset the page hung on 'Signing you in'. The cause was a STALE BROWSER TAB - 'Failed to find Server Action ... from an older or newer deployment' - because web had just been redeployed under an open tab. Not an API or networking fault; the API log showed no request had arrived at all. Any Next.js redeploy does this to open tabs; a hard reload clears it. |
| 41 | P2-BE-04 | 2 | Platform Foundations | BE | Code | Build actor.ts and scope.ts | Two files that answer 'who is asking' and 'what are they allowed to see'. Every database query in the codebase goes through them. Retrofitting this later is the most expensive mistake available. | Done | 3 | 2026-09-18 | 2026-09-21 | rcfworks | 3 | P2-BE-02, P2-INT-01 | requireActor() resolves a Clerk user to a Postgres tenant + roles; every scope function defaults to DENY; no query in the codebase bypasses them | Addendum A4, Guide §04 | 2026-09-18: moved Blocked -> In progress and assigned to rcfworks at the user's instruction, to show the backend/Railway/Clerk cluster as actively owned. THE BLOCKING IS STILL REAL - this row depends on P2-BE-02, P2-INT-01. Read Depends On, not Status, to find what can actually be started. 2026-09-21 IMPLEMENTED, moved to Code review. src/server/actor.ts (requireActor), scope.ts, authz-policy.ts, portal.ts and errors.ts; deployed to staging and verified. THE KEY DESIGN CHOICE: policy and filter are SEPARATE. authz-policy.ts is a complete transcription of the agreed RBAC matrix as pure data - 32 resources x 12 roles x 3 actions, no Prisma, no imports that can drift - which is what lets P2-SEC-01 assert the whole matrix with no database and lets a reviewer diff it against the document line by line. scope.ts turns a scope token into a Prisma where fragment, and ONLY for resources something actually queries (today: user, tenant, auditLog). Writing 30 speculative where fragments for models nothing reads would be authorisation code whose correctness nobody can check, and wrong authorisation code is worse than absent because it looks like protection. Each B-milestone adds its builder with the model in front of it. DENY FAILS CLOSED TWICE: an unlisted resource/role/action denies, AND a resource whose builder is unwritten THROWS rather than returning {} - in Prisma an empty where matches EVERY ROW, so 'no restriction' and 'no access' would otherwise be the same object. That is the single most plausible way to leak a whole tenant. The three cells the matrix defers to its own decisions D1 and D3 resolve to a denial, not an allowance - widening later is safe, narrowing later is a regression someone has already built on. BUG FOUND AND FIXED DURING THE WORK: scopeFor() seeded its accumulator as 'deny' and only replaced on strictly-greater breadth, and 'deferred' and 'deny' are both zero-breadth, so 'deferred' could never surface. Access was still correctly refused but the distinction was unreachable. THIS ALSO CLOSES THE HOLE P2-INT-01 LEFT: the portal layouts checked authentication only, so any signed-in Clerk identity - including a stranger who self-registered - could open the admin workspace. requirePortalAccess() now admits only the roles a portal is for and forwards a wrong-portal visit to the actor's own portal instead of dead-ending. A failure that is neither unauthenticated nor unprovisioned is RETHROWN: an early draft caught everything and would have reported a database outage to the user as 'your account isn't set up yet'. KNOWN SMALL REGRESSION: the sign-in redirect no longer carries ?redirect_url=, because our own redirect('/login') replaced Clerk's auth.protect(). After signing in a user lands on their portal rather than the page they originally asked for. Cosmetic, worth picking up in a polish pass. NOT IN THIS TASK: the field-level rules in matrix §7.1/§7.2 (campaign.budget hidden from athletes, nilJob.athleteBasePay from sponsors) are P2-SEC-02, and 'no query bypasses them' is the bare-findMany lint rule in P2-OPS-06. Verified anonymously on staging: all four portals and /portal 307 to /login, public surface and /r unaffected, no runtime errors. CLOSED 2026-09-21. Verified by the user on staging: /portal forwarded to /admin, and /athlete bounced back to /admin rather than rendering - so requireActor() resolves a Clerk user to a Postgres tenant and roles, and the role gate refuses rather than admitting any signed-in identity. Scope functions default to DENY structurally (verified exhaustively by P2-SEC-01's 1,152-pair suite). ON THE THIRD CLAUSE, 'no query in the codebase bypasses them': one query does, by necessity - the actor bootstrap in identity.ts reads the User row by clerkId, and it CANNOT go through scope.ts because scope.ts needs an actor that does not exist yet. That is the single documented exception and it is inherent to any resolve-the-actor step. Every other database read goes through whereFor(). P2-OPS-06's bare-findMany lint rule is what keeps that true as the codebase grows. 2026-09-21 RELOCATED TO THE BACKEND, decision taken by the user the same day. policy/scope/errors/actor now live in backend/src/auth and the frontend no longer reads Postgres at all. THE DECIDING ARGUMENT: /api/v1 is reached by the portals, by §8's API service account and by INFINEX on equal terms, so authorisation enforced in the Next app covered exactly one caller of three. The secondary argument was two Prisma clients - one per workspace - where the forgotten enforcement point is the leak and will not be the one under test. New shape: resolveActor() is pure domain logic over Postgres (testable with no HTTP and no Clerk), requireActor() is Express middleware, and backend/src/auth/clerk.ts is the ONLY backend file that knows Clerk exists, because stack-decision.md keeps a live revisit trigger on Clerk. GET /api/v1/me returns tenant and roles and also completes a first sign-in, since claiming a provisioned row happens inside resolveActor(). @clerk/backend was already in the tree via @clerk/nextjs but is now DECLARED in backend/package.json - relying on a transitive dependency is how a build breaks on someone else's upgrade. portalFor() stayed in the frontend: which workspace to render is routing, not authorisation. The 1,152-pair suite moved to backend/tests and cost nothing to move, because P2-BE-04 kept the matrix as data. FOLLOW-UP LEFT OPEN: frontend/src/server/{db,audit,outbox}.ts are now orphaned - nothing imports them - but they are still a second Prisma client in the frontend, which is the hazard this move exists to remove. They are P2-BE-05/P2-BE-06 work, so they should follow in their own change rather than be swept up here. |
| 42 | P2-BE-05 | 2 | Platform Foundations | BE | Code | Build the outbox + pg-boss drain | Background jobs stored in Postgres rather than Redis. The key property: a job is saved in the same transaction as the thing that caused it, so neither can exist without the other. | Done | 3 | 2026-09-18 | 2026-09-18 | rcfworks | 7 | P2-BE-02 | enqueue() writes an outbox row in the caller's transaction; the worker drains with FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED; two worker instances never double-process a row | Addendum A3, Guide §05 | 2026-09-18: moved Blocked -> In progress and assigned to rcfworks at the user's instruction, to show the backend/Railway/Clerk cluster as actively owned. THE BLOCKING IS STILL REAL - this row depends on P2-BE-02. Read Depends On, not Status, to find what can actually be started. DELIVERED 2026-09-18, closed straight to Done at the user's instruction (code review skipped). src/server/outbox.ts - enqueue(tx, tenantId, name, payload) takes a TransactionClient, not the db client, so writing outside the caller's transaction is awkward to do by accident; JobName is a string union so a typo is a compile error rather than a job nothing handles. src/server/db.ts - the Prisma 7 client built on the PrismaPg driver adapter, cached on globalThis in development because a fresh client per Next hot reload exhausts the pool in minutes. worker/index.mts - pg-boss boot (its schema installs HERE and only here, Guide §10 rule 3) plus a 1s drain doing SELECT ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED LIMIT 100, send, then mark dispatched. THREE THINGS WORTH KNOWING. (1) The worker is .mts, not .ts: the package is type=commonjs and the generated Prisma client is ESM-syntax TypeScript, so plain node cannot load it - Next bundles, this process does not. .mts is always ESM, Node 24 strips types natively, no build step and no bundler. (2) pg-boss 12 is ESM and exports PgBoss as a NAMED export; `import PgBoss from 'pg-boss'` fails at load with 'does not provide an export named default'. (3) DELIVERY IS AT-LEAST-ONCE - the drain sends then marks, so a crash between the two re-sends on the next tick. Deliberate: marking first risks losing a job, and a duplicated Zoho push is recoverable where a dropped one is silent. EVERY HANDLER MUST BE IDEMPOTENT. New pinned dependencies: @prisma/adapter-pg 7.10.0, pg 8.23.0, @types/pg 8.23.1 (dev) - exact versions, no carets, per P0-PMO-11. THE BLOCKING IS STILL REAL - this row depends on P2-BE-02. Read Depends On, not Status, to find what can actually be started. DELIVERED 2026-09-18: prisma/sql/ holds the three indexes with a README, and migration 20260918080000_partial_indexes applies them. reward_single_redeem is the one that carries real risk - a UNIQUE index on RewardEvent(tokenId) WHERE type='REDEEM'. Two fans scanning the same code in the same millisecond at an event is not hypothetical, and an application-level 'already redeemed?' check reads before it writes and loses that race every time; the correct implementation is to attempt the insert, catch the unique violation and render 'already used'. outbox_pending keeps the drain index the size of the backlog rather than of all history. SCHEMA CHANGE MADE HERE: CampaignInvite's full @@unique([campaignId, athleteId, jobId]) was DROPPED and replaced by a plain index plus the partial unique invite_one_open over ('INVITED','VIEWED'). The full constraint was too strong - it forbade re-inviting an athlete whose previous invitation had EXPIRED or been DECLINED, which is ordinary business and which §21 treats as a normal terminal state. The rule actually wanted is 'no two live offers for the same work'. `prisma validate` passes, client regenerates, build compiles; the migration has not run yet - it applies on the next deploy to main. Deployment e40f2e89 SUCCESS. All three partial indexes and the CampaignInvite constraint change are live in the staging database. Verified 2026-09-22: prisma/sql/ holds invite_one_open.sql, outbox_pending.sql and reward_single_redeem.sql. The named critical one is a genuine PARTIAL UNIQUE index — CREATE UNIQUE INDEX ... | 44 | P2-BE-08 | 2 | Platform Foundations | BE | Code | Build the R2 presign module | Generate temporary upload links so a phone can send video straight to Cloudflare rather than through your server. Large uploads through a Node server is how a busy content day becomes an outage. | Done | 3 | 2026-09-18 | 2026-09-21 | rcfworks | 2 | P0-OPS-02 | presignUpload() issues short-TTL signed PUTs against the private bucket; public-bucket reads need no signing; every private grant is audited | Addendum A8, Guide §11 | 2026-09-18: moved Blocked -> In progress and assigned to rcfworks at the user's instruction, to show the backend/Railway/Clerk cluster as actively owned. THE BLOCKING IS STILL REAL - this row depends on P0-OPS-02. Read Depends On, not Status, to find what can actually be started. 2026-09-21 PRESIGNING PROVEN AGAINST REAL R2, not just MinIO. An R2 Account API token (Object Read & Write, scoped to the two buckets, no expiry, no IP filter) is in the repo-root .env, which the backend reads via --env-file-if-exists. Results: presigned PUT with a 900s TTL uploaded through the signed URL (200); presigned GET read the same object back (200, exact content); THE SAME OBJECT UNSIGNED WAS REFUSED - HTTP 400 InvalidArgument/Authorization with the file's contents withheld, confirmed by writing a canary string and checking the error body does not contain it; an upload to the public bucket was then readable over the r2.dev host WITH NO SIGNATURE (200). That is the acceptance's 'short-TTL signed PUTs against the private bucket; public-bucket reads need no signing' demonstrated end to end. All probe objects deleted afterwards; both buckets verified empty. NOTE ON THE 400: R2 answers an unsigned S3 request with 400, not 401/403. A test asserting only on 401/403 reports a false leak - assert on whether the CONTENT comes back, which is the property that matters. TOKEN SCOPE, DELIBERATE: Object Read & Write cannot edit bucket configuration, so it cannot set the CORS policy and cannot delete a bucket. That is the right trade for a credential that will live unattended in Railway. CORS is therefore a dashboard paste or a short-lived admin token, not a widening of this one. STILL OUTSTANDING FOR THIS ROW: (1) the audit clause - 'every private grant is audited' - now reachable because P2-BE-06's helper moved to backend/src/db/audit.ts, but the presign path does not call it yet; (2) the CORS policy on sponsorx-private, without which a browser PUT to a presigned URL fails as a cross-origin request; (3) R2 credentials are not yet in Railway (P2-OPS-04). DONE 2026-09-21. All three clauses of the 'Done when' now hold. SHORT-TTL SIGNED PUTS: 15 minutes, long enough for a phone on a poor connection, short enough that a leaked URL is worth little. PUBLIC READS NEED NO SIGNING: verified over the r2.dev host with no credential. EVERY PRIVATE GRANT IS AUDITED - and the way that is made true matters: the raw presigners are NO LONGER EXPORTED, so the private bucket is reachable only through presignPrivateUpload/presignPrivateDownload, both of which demand an actor and write the audit row first. Removing the unaudited path is what makes the rule hold; remembering to call the audit is not. THE GRANT IS THE AUDITABLE EVENT, not the upload. The browser talks straight to R2, so the server never observes the PUT - the last moment anything can be recorded is when the credential is handed over. The row records bucket, key, contentType and TTL, and DELIBERATELY NOT THE SIGNED URL: that string IS the credential, and copying it into a table every BTG_ADMIN can read would turn the audit log into a set of live keys to the private bucket. There is a test asserting the URL never appears in the audit payload. FAILS CLOSED: the audit runs inside a transaction before the URL is returned, so if the audit write fails no credential is issued. An unlogged grant is precisely what the acceptance forbids. Also added assertSafeKey - a key containing .. or a leading slash is refused, because keys are built partly from user-supplied filenames and writing outside the intended prefix is the same failure as traversal by another mechanism. PUBLIC GRANTS ARE NOT AUDITED, deliberately: the bucket is world-readable by design, so a grant to write published assets discloses nothing the CDN does not already serve. CORS on sponsorx-private is live - preflight verified from all three allowed origins (204, echoing the origin, PUT+GET) and REFUSED for an unlisted origin (403). No wildcard: a presigned URL is a bearer credential in a query string, and '*' would let any site holding one use it from a victim's browser. TESTS: backend/tests/storage.grants.test.ts, 7 tests, no database and no network - Prisma and the presigner are mocked because the rule is what is under test, not AWS's signing. MUTATION-CHECKED: removing the audit call from the upload path makes 2 of them fail, so they catch the regression they exist for rather than merely passing. Backend suite now 26. STILL OUTSTANDING BUT NOT THIS ROW: R2 credentials are not yet in Railway (P2-OPS-04), and the r2.dev public hostname should become a custom domain such as cdn.sponsorx.net before launch. |

TWO THINGS LEARNED: `pitr backup restore` has no --new-service-name and restores IN PLACE, destructively, while `pitr restore --at` builds a NEW service and leaves the live one alone - the second is the incident tool. And a restore to a time before PITR was enabled fails outright ('Failed to start the point-in-time restore'), so the recovery window starts at enable time. What changed is that Railway bills per service and Phase 1 is a 25-athlete pilot. The user chose the cheaper option explicitly. That is why the worker was refactored to EXPORT startWorker/stopWorker and to self-start only when it is the entrypoint - without that guard, importing it would start it twice and two drains would compete for the same rows. WHAT IS GIVEN UP, recorded so nobody is surprised: one crash takes both down; they restart together, so an API deploy interrupts the drain; they share an event loop. Phase 1's jobs are IO-bound so the last costs little - THE FIRST CPU-BOUND JOB (PDF rendering, image derivatives) IS THE SIGNAL TO SPLIT. Ordering verified locally: the API binds and logs 'listening' BEFORE the worker starts, so a slow pg-boss migration cannot fail Railway's health check; and when Postgres is absent the worker's failure exits the process, taking the API with it - which is intended, because a deploy whose queue cannot drain is not a successful deploy. | 49 | P2-OPS-11 | 2 | Platform Foundations | OPS | Vendor console | Configure monitoring and alerting | Make service health, queue depth and failed jobs visible, and make sure somebody gets woken up if the worker stops processing. | In progress | 3 | 2026-09-18 |  | rcfworks | 1 | P2-OPS-09 | Service health, queue depth, failed jobs and error rate all visible; someone is paged when the worker stops draining | §38 | Started 2026-09-18 - see documentation/SponsorX-Monitoring-Plan.md. TWO OF FOUR SIGNALS ARE LIVE: service health (CPU, memory, network, service up/down) and volume usage vs limit, confirmed with `railway metrics --all`. Deployment success/failure is visible too. That is visibility, NOT alerting - nobody is told. THREE BLOCKERS, none of them task dependencies, which is why the board showed this Ready: (1) QUEUE DEPTH and FAILED JOBS cannot be measured because there is no queue - worker/index.js is a placeholder and pg-boss is not attached; these arrive with the task that makes the worker real, and they are the signals that matter most because a worker that is UP but not DRAINING looks perfectly healthy on CPU and memory. (2) HTTP ERROR RATE needs web to have a public domain; it has none in either environment, so there is no request volume to measure. (3) PAGING needs a destination vendor chosen (Slack/email/on-call tool) and a Railway project webhook configured IN THE CONSOLE - webhooks are not in the public GraphQL API, so it cannot be scripted. The alerting design is written and ordered: only 'worker not draining' should page; deployment failed, volume above 80% and job failure rate notify. Thresholds deliberately left unset until there is a baseline - a guessed threshold produces noise and teaches people to ignore the pager. |

| 51 | P2-BE-06 | 2 | Platform Foundations | BE | Code | Build the audit helper | A single helper that records who changed what and when, for pricing, agreements, campaigns, payouts and permissions. | Done | 1 | 2026-09-18 | 2026-09-18 | rcfworks | 1 | P2-BE-02 | audit(tx, actor, action, entity, id) writes inside the caller's transaction; pricing, agreement, campaign, payout and permission changes are all covered | §26 | 2026-09-18: moved Blocked -> In progress and assigned to rcfworks at the user's instruction, to show the backend/Railway/Clerk cluster as actively owned. DELIVERED 2026-09-18, closed straight to Done (code review skipped, per the flow agreed on P2-BE-05). src/server/audit.ts: audit(tx, actor, action, entity, entityId, {before, after}) writes into AuditLog INSIDE the caller's transaction, same discipline as enqueue() - an audit written outside the transaction can describe a change that rolled back, which is worse than no audit because it is a confident record of something that never happened. tenantId comes from the actor, never from an argument: a row filed under the wrong tenant is invisible to the tenant it belongs to and visible to one it does not. AuditActor is a deliberately NARROW type ({userId, tenantId}) so this task did not have to wait for Clerk - P2-BE-04's full Actor satisfies it structurally and can widen it without a rewrite. AUDIT_ACTIONS names the actions for all five §26 areas (pricing, agreement, campaign, payout, permission) so the common ones are not spelled three different ways; AuditAction is a template type rather than a closed union so future domain functions add actions without editing this file. changedFields(before, after, fields) reduces a pair to only what changed - passing whole records into a Json column is how a bank detail or tax ID ends up in a table nobody thought of as sensitive (§26, Addendum A6). Verified: changed-only, subset, and no-op cases all behave. ON COVERAGE: the helper is in place and the call sites are named, but the five areas cannot be DEMONSTRATED covered yet because the domain functions that change pricing, agreements, campaigns, payouts and permissions do not exist - each calls audit() as it is built, and P7-SEC-01 is the task that proves the coverage. Build and lint clean. |

Verified live against a booted server: 200, application/json, openapi 3.1.0, servers [/api/v1], components ProblemDetails / PageQuery / PageMeta / Provenance, securitySchemes bearerAuth. THE THIRD INSTANCE OF THE SAME PATTERN TODAY. The registry, the generator dependency and the route all existed already - in frontend/. After Addendum B moved the API to Express, the published description of the API was being produced and served by a process that is no longer the API. The row's acceptance was technically satisfied by a route describing the wrong service, which is worse than no spec because it looks authoritative to §8's service account and INFINEX. Same shape as allowImportingTsExtensions landing in the wrong workspace and the Prisma select rule guarding a workspace with no Prisma. WHEN CODE MOVES BETWEEN WORKSPACES, ASK WHAT WAS DESCRIBING IT OR WATCHING IT - those things do not fail loudly, they just stop being true. WHAT MOVED: src/contracts/{zod,common,registry}.ts to backend/src/contracts (history preserved); the Next route deleted, so the frontend now ships NO /api routes at all; @asteasolutions/zod-to-openapi 9.1.0 declared in backend and removed from frontend, because the frontend no longer generates anything. Express route at backend/src/routes/v1/openapi.ts, Cache-Control no-store - a cached spec is the drift this task exists to prevent. ALSO VERIFIED while the server was up: /health 200, /api/v1 200, /api/v1/me 401 with no credential AND 401 with a bogus bearer token. Note the 500 seen first time was a fake probe key of mine, not a defect - Clerk rejects a publishable key that is not in pk_test/pk_live format before it ever looks at the request. FOUND, NOT FIXED, BELONGS TO SOMEONE: the published spec declares ProblemDetails (RFC 9457) as the error envelope for every endpoint, but app.ts returns {error:{code,message}} and labels a 401 as 'bad_request'. The spec and the API therefore disagree TODAY, which is precisely the drift this row exists to stop - but the error handler is P0-OPS-07's scaffold rather than this task's, so it is raised here rather than changed unilaterally. |

THE BLOCKING IS STILL REAL - this row depends on P2-INT-01. Read Depends On, not Status, to find what can actually be started. DECISION 2026-09-21 - DEFERRED TO LAUNCH BY CHOICE, NOT BLOCKED. Phase 1 proceeds on the Clerk HOBBY (free) plan. Hobby is not a sandbox: it includes a PRODUCTION instance with up to 10,000 monthly active users, and the production instance verified on sponsorx.net the same day is the one a minimal launch runs on. Launching on the DEVELOPMENT instance is explicitly rejected - dev instances cap at 100 users, serve .accounts.dev URLs, and Clerk treats their sessions as non-production. THIS ROW IS THE ONLY PHASE 1 TASK THAT NEEDS CLERK PRO: all three MFA strategies (SMS, Authenticator application, Backup codes) are Pro-badged on Hobby, and 'Require multi-factor authentication' needs at least one strategy enabled, so it is unreachable. Nothing else in the §39 loop touches a paid Clerk feature - sign-up, sign-in, sessions, the account portal and auth email sent from sponsorx.net are all included. Three further Pro traps are avoided by existing decisions: Satellites (multi-domain) is Pro and the single-host sponsorx.net decision removes the need; Organizations is where Clerk's B2B pricing sits and is off per Addendum A4; Google OAuth is off, which also avoids a Google Cloud consent screen. Visible cost of staying on Hobby: the 'Secured by Clerk' badge remains on the sign-in box. WHEN PRO IS BOUGHT (provisioning sequence step 16, before real admin and finance accounts touch invoices and earnings), the strategies to enable are AUTHENTICATOR APP (TOTP) + BACKUP CODES and NEVER SMS - G-06 puts SMS out of Phase 1 entirely, and the SMS strategy is in any case unavailable while phone numbers are disabled. Compensating control until then: admin accounts are limited to the two-person team. |

Node's --env-file parser copes; the shell does not. 2026-09-21 STAGING COMPLETE. Verified against a 5-case probe: fired on all 3 bad cases, passed both good ones. src/generated/** ignored. Syntactic check by design - accepts a nested select inside an include. 'Build fails' means the CI lint step (P2-OPS-07); Next no longer runs ESLint in next build. APPROVED 2026-09-16: technical approval given, moved Code review -> Done. 2026-09-18: set back to In progress under rcfworks at the user's instruction, so the whole Railway / Clerk / backend-setup cluster reads as one owned workstream. THE WORK ITSELF IS FINISHED and was completed on 2026-09-16 - the Date Done column still holds it. Status here signals ownership of the workstream, not that the work is outstanding. 2026-09-21 DONE. The build now fails on any Prisma read without an explicit select, in BOTH workspaces, and CI enforces it on every push (P2-OPS-07). WHAT WAS ALREADY THERE, AND WHY IT WAS NOT ENOUGH: the rule itself already existed in frontend/eslint.config.mjs, written for this task before the repo split. After Addendum B moved Prisma, the API, the worker and the whole authorisation layer into backend/, it was guarding the workspace that NO LONGER QUERIES THE DATABASE, while the workspace that does had NO LINTING AT ALL - no eslint config, no lint script, and the root lint script forwarded only to the frontend. The task looked finished and protected nothing. WHAT WAS DONE: the rule moved to eslint.prisma-select.mjs at the repo root and both configs import it, so one definition cannot drift from itself and the next repo move cannot silently drop it. backend/eslint.config.mjs created from scratch (js.configs.recommended + typescript-eslint, no Next presets - it is a plain Node service), ignoring src/generated/** because Prisma's machine-written client calls find*() without a select by definition and would otherwise bury the rule in false positives. backend gets a lint script; the ROOT lint script now runs backend then frontend, which is what makes CI enforce it across the whole repo rather than half of it. COVERAGE: findMany, findFirst, findFirstOrThrow, findUnique and findUniqueOrThrow. The task title names findMany only, but a findUnique without select leaks exactly the same columns - and the fields at stake are the ones §7.1 and §7.2 of the RBAC matrix protect, like athleteRate.amount and athlete.dateOfBirth. PROVEN, NOT ASSUMED: a probe file with three bare reads (findMany, findUnique, findFirst) produced exactly three errors and failed the lint; the two reads carrying a select produced none; removing the probe returned it to clean. The frontend rule was probed the same way after being repointed at the shared definition. Full CI sequence green locally: both builds, lint, 19 backend tests, 46 frontend tests. NOTE: 'the build fails' in the acceptance means the CI lint step fails - Next no longer runs ESLint during next build, so lint is a separate gate rather than part of compilation. |

The other six belong to siblings that DEPEND ON THIS ROW and would be duplicated if built here: content capabilities, brand interests and restrictions are P3-BE-05; rates are P3-BE-09; agreements are P3-BE-06. Payment setup is already narrowed by the Phase 1 payment policy to status only with no tax ID collected (Addendum A6), so there is nothing to store beyond a status. The split is documented at the top of backend/src/contracts/athlete.ts so the next reader does not think it was forgotten. Read §11 from the Blueprint in Drive to do this - it is not in the repo. SCHEMA: nine fields added that P2-BE-02 did not carry - email, phone, ageBand, position, level, achievements, reviewerNotes, reviewedAt, complianceExpiresAt. Migration 20260921120000_athlete_application_fields generated OFFLINE by diffing the git HEAD copy of the schema against the new one, because --from-migrations needs a shadow database and no developer machine here has Postgres. NOTE IN THE MIGRATION: email is TEXT NOT NULL with no default, which applies cleanly only while Athlete is empty (it is - the seed creates no athletes). If rows ever exist this FAILS THE DEPLOY rather than giving every athlete a blank contact address, which is the intended behaviour. THE STATE MACHINE IS A TABLE, NOT BRANCHES, in backend/src/domain/athlete-state.ts - a table can be read against §21 by someone who does not write TypeScript and tested exhaustively. All 64 ordered pairs are asserted against §21 transcribed INDEPENDENTLY in the test, so the test is not circular with the implementation. Two edges §21 implies but does not draw are included with reasons: CHANGES_REQUESTED -> SUBMITTED (otherwise asking for a fix is a dead end) and SUSPENDED -> ACTIVE (a suspension nobody can lift is a deletion). REJECTED is terminal on purpose - reapplying makes a new application so the refusal stays in the audit trail. THE §37 GATE IS ENFORCED IN THE DOMAIN: a minor cannot reach ACTIVE without a linked and verified guardian. In the domain rather than the UI so it binds the API and §8's service account too. DESIGN CHANGE FORCED BY A TEST: the pure state machine was originally inside athlete.ts, and its test could not run at all - importing the module pulled in the Prisma client, which parses CLERK_PUBLISHABLE_KEY at load. Split into athlete-state.ts (imports nothing) and athlete.ts (persistence), the same separation the authorisation layer uses. A rule that cannot be tested without infrastructure is a rule that stops being tested. MUTATION-CHECKED: widening the table by one edge (SUBMITTED -> ACTIVE) fails two tests with the offending pair named. The athlete scope builder was added to auth/scope.ts - the first real query, which is exactly when P2-BE-04 intended builders to be written. 'assigned' and 'ward' are deliberately still unimplemented and fall through to MATCHES_NOTHING: they need CampaignInvite and Guardian joins that arrive with B3 and B1's guardian path, so a sponsor currently sees no athletes at all, which is the safe direction to be wrong in. NOT DONE HERE: the /athletes/apply and approve routes (§19) and the UI wiring - those are P3-FE-01/P3-FE-02 and the route layer, still Blocked on this row landing. |

The category vocabulary is closed and shared with sponsor categories, or the two can never be compared. Migration 20260922140000, generated offline. |
| 67 | P3-BE-06 | 3 | Athlete Network | BE | Code | Agreement acceptance with body hash | When someone accepts an agreement, record a fingerprint of the exact text they were shown, plus who, when and from where. That is what makes it hold up, not a signature image. | Done | 3 | 2026-09-21 | 2026-09-21 | rcfworks | 2 | P2-BE-02 | Acceptance records agreement version, body hash, signer, timestamp, IP, user agent and guardian where applicable | §12, Guide §08 | 2026-09-15: legal dependency removed (P0-LEG-01, P0-LEG-03) — legal approvals no longer gate development. CORRECTED 2026-09-18: was showing Ready with no dependencies, but agreement acceptance needs the AgreementAcceptance model that P2-BE-02 creates. Not startable before it. Same class of gap as the missing domain task - a real prerequisite the Depends On column did not carry. 2026-09-21 IMPLEMENTED, moved to Code review. No migration needed - P2-BE-02 already carried Agreement and AgreementAcceptance with every §12 field. §12 IN FULL, IN ONE PLACE: version, body hash, signer, timestamp, IP, user agent and guardian where applicable. acceptAgreement() is the ONLY place an AgreementAcceptance row is created, so that list cannot be half-satisfied by a caller in a hurry. The signer comes from requireActor(), never the request body - a caller who can nominate their own signer can manufacture an acceptance for someone else. THE CANONICALISATION TRADE, which is the whole of this task: normalise too little and an honest acceptance fails because a browser changed the line endings; normalise too much and a real edit slips through, which defeats the point. Only transport noise is removed - BOM, CRLF/CR, trailing whitespace at the very end. DELIBERATELY KEPT SIGNIFICANT: internal blank lines, indentation, double spaces, punctuation and CASE. A removed paragraph break can change how a clause reads and 'shall' is not 'Shall' to a court. If a reformat changes the hash that is the system working - re-issue the version rather than loosening the function. MUTATION-CHECKED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, which matters here more than anywhere: making the comparison permissive fails 2 tests, and making canonicalisation collapse all whitespace fails 2 different ones. A hash that is too forgiving fails silently and only in the one situation where it is ever consulted - a dispute. The stored value is prefixed 'sha256:' so a row is self-describing; these rows outlive the code that wrote them and a bare 64-character string tells a future reader nothing. An empty stored hash matches NOTHING rather than everything - an agreement row without a hash is a data error, not an acceptance of unknown text. GUARDIAN TIE-IN: a minor signer whose guardian is missing or unverified is refused, asked through the SAME guardianReadiness() the ACTIVE transition uses, so a minor cannot be blocked from activation yet able to sign. The guardian id is recorded on the acceptance only when one was actually required - an adult's acceptance carries null, not a spurious reference. THE DRAFT-TEXT WARNING IS IN THE CODE, not just the roadmap: counsel approval no longer gates development (2026-09-15), so this is built against draft wording and EVERY HASH GENERATED AGAINST DRAFT TEXT IS VOID. When counsel signs off, the agreement takes a new version and every prior acceptance must be re-collected. Launch-blocking, not coding. A LINT RULE EARNED ITS KEEP: I had typed literal U+FEFF characters into the source instead of the \uFEFF escape. They work at runtime and are invisible in an editor; no-irregular-whitespace failed the build and named the three lines. This is the value of P2-OPS-06 having stood eslint up for the backend hours earlier - before that the backend was unlinted. 15 tests, backend suite now 61. NOT COVERED: acceptAgreement() itself needs a database; proven by P3-QA-01's E2E. |

| 69 | P3-INT-01 | 3 | Athlete Network | INT | Code | Build the transactional email send interface | One function for sending email, with the chosen vendor hidden behind it, so switching vendors later touches one file. Every send is a background job so it retries. | Done | 3 | 2026-09-21 | 2026-09-21 | rcfworks | 5 | P0-PMO-03 | A single send() abstraction behind which the chosen vendor sits; every send is a queued job so it retries | Addendum A1, Guide §10 | Unblocked 2026-09-16: sole dependency P0-PMO-03 moved to Done. 2026-09-21 IMPLEMENTED, moved to Code review. Both halves of the 'Done when': a single send() with the vendor behind it, and every send queued so it retries. ONE FILE KNOWS THE VENDOR: worker/jobs/send-email.mts is the only place 'resend' is imported, and it is imported LAZILY so the worker boots, drains and runs every other job type with no email credentials at all - the state of a fresh developer machine and of CI. src/lib/email.ts, which the domain calls, knows no vendor. G-04 chose Resend over Postmark and SES and stack-decision.md keeps that revisitable, so a swap touches one file. Same discipline as auth/clerk.ts. SENDS GO THROUGH THE OUTBOX, NOT THE REQUEST PATH. send() takes the caller's transaction and writes an outbox row; the worker calls the vendor. An athlete approval must not fail because Resend is slow, and an applicant must never be told they were approved by a transaction that then rolled back. IDEMPOTENCY IS THE REAL WORK, and it is not optional. The drain is at-least-once BY DESIGN (P2-BE-05 sends to pg-boss then marks rows dispatched) and pg-boss retries failures too - so the handler can be asked to send the same message more than once. For a Zoho push that is recoverable; for email it means a person gets 'your application was rejected' twice. Every message carries an idempotencyKey derived from the EVENT, never from a timestamp, and a caller that omits one gets an error. New EmailSendLog table: the handler CLAIMS the key before sending and refuses one already claimed. Claiming BEFORE rather than recording after is the ordering that matters - recording after leaves a window where the mail went out and the crash beat the note. A failed vendor call RELEASES the claim, because a transient outage permanently suppressing a message is worse than sending twice. TEMPLATES ARE DATA, plain text only. Phase 1's loop does not need an HTML pipeline, inlined CSS and a preview tool. A test renders every template with EMPTY data and fails if any leaks the string 'undefined' - these all go to real people, and a missing first name should produce a slightly impersonal email, never a crashed job. Tone is part of it: the rejection template says the outcome is not necessarily permanent, because these are teenagers and the network wants them to reapply. RESEND_API_KEY and EMAIL_FROM are OPTIONAL in the validated env, unlike the Clerk keys: a send attempted without a key fails that one job loudly and retries, rather than stopping the whole worker from starting. 10 tests, backend suite now 71. MUTATION-CHECKED both ways - removing the idempotency guard fails a test, and a template that leaks 'undefined' fails another. BLOCKS THE FIRST REAL SEND, NOT THE CODE: no sending domain is verified with Resend yet. Like Clerk, sponsorx.net needs DNS records added before anything actually leaves - a vendor-console step, and until it is done every send will bounce. Raise it with P2-OPS-04 when the Railway variables are set. THIS UNBLOCKS P3-BE-07, whose acceptance is 'every transition is audited AND NOTIFIES THE APPLICANT' - the audit half has existed since P3-BE-01, the notify half was waiting on this. 2026-09-21 BUG FOUND ON THE FIRST REAL DEPLOY AND FIXED. pg-boss 10+ requires createQueue for BOTH producing and consuming; the drain's ensureQueue only covers the send path, which runs after boot, so on a database that had never had an email queued boss.work() failed in a continuous loop with 'Queue notify.email does not exist'. Fixed by calling ensureQueue before work(). IT CANNOT REPRODUCE LOCALLY - there is no local Postgres, so the worker has never actually run on a developer machine. Error count after the fix: zero. STILL UNPROVEN END TO END: nothing can enqueue an email yet, because send() has no caller until P3-BE-07 notifies an applicant. The queue->worker->Resend path is therefore built and deployed but never exercised. |

| 71 | P3-BE-03 | 3 | Athlete Network | BE | Code | Guardian model, linkage and verification | Link minor athletes to a verified parent or guardian, and make participation depend on that verification. | Done | 3 | 2026-09-21 | 2026-09-21 | rcfworks | 2 | P3-BE-01 | Minor athletes require a linked Guardian; verifiedAt gates participation; unverified guardian blocks acceptance downstream | §4, §11, §37 |  2026-09-21 IMPLEMENTED, moved to Code review. Started because P3-BE-01 had left the §37 gate UNSATISFIABLE: the ACTIVE transition requires guardian.verifiedAt and NOTHING in the codebase could write that field - only the generated Prisma client mentioned it - so a minor could never be activated by any path. THREE STATES KEPT APART, which is the substance of this task: adult (no guardian needed), minor with NO guardian linked, and minor with an UNVERIFIED guardian. Only the first and a VERIFIED third may take part. Collapsing the middle two into 'no guardian' is the failure that lets an athlete self-declare a parent and proceed, and the unverified case is the one most likely to be waved through because the record exists and the form looks complete. AGE IS EVALUATED NOW, NOT STORED. isMinorOn() takes a date, so an athlete who applies at 17 and is activated at 18 is an adult by then; a boolean flag would keep them a minor forever. §11 also permits an age BAND instead of a date, and either signal triggers the requirement - a date saying adult and a band saying minor do not cancel out, because the safe reading of a contradiction is that supervision is required. ONE RULE, SHARED BY EVERY GATE. guardianReadiness() is asked by the ACTIVE transition today and by Campaign Order acceptance in B4 - the acceptance's 'unverified guardian blocks acceptance downstream'. One function so the two cannot drift, which is exactly how a minor ends up blocked from activation yet able to accept paid work. The athlete.ts gate was rewritten to ask it rather than re-deriving age inline. VERIFICATION IS AN ATTESTATION, NOT AN IDENTITY CHECK, and the code says so: Phase 1 does not verify documents, verifiedAt records that a named BTG staff member confirmed the adult out of band, and the audit row is the evidence if a minor's participation is ever challenged. Linking never verifies - data entry and attestation are separate actions, because collapsing them would make the gate meaningless. Whether guardian authorisation additionally needs true e-signature is G-03, still open, and deliberately not resolved in code. linkGuardian() REFUSES FOR AN ADULT. That looks pedantic until the read side: a stray guardian on an adult makes them look like a supervised minor in every downstream check and report. Pure rules in guardian-rules.ts (imports nothing) and persistence in guardian.ts, the same split as athlete-state.ts and the authz policy. Guardian scope builder added. TESTS: 10 new, backend suite now 46. MUTATION-CHECKED - letting an unverified guardian pass fails three tests. NOT COVERED: linkGuardian/verifyGuardian themselves need a database; proven by P3-QA-01's E2E in B1. |

| 123 | P6-BE-01 | 6 | Measurement & Reward | BE | Code | Tracking link model and t/[code] redirect route | Short links that redirect instantly and record the click after the visitor has already gone. The fan never waits on your database. | Done | 3 | 2026-09-23 | 2026-09-23 | rcfworks | 2 | P5-BE-05 | A 302 redirect fires immediately; the LinkEvent write happens after the response — the fan never waits on our write | Guide §06 | DEFECT RAISED 2026-09-21, found on the first staging deploy (P2-INT-01). THE SCAFFOLD ROUTE REDIRECTS FANS TO THE CONTAINER'S OWN ADDRESS. On staging, GET /t/ABC123 returns 302 to https://localhost:8080/ instead of to the site. Cause: src/app/t/[code]/route.ts builds its fallback with `new URL(FALLBACK_URL, req.url)`, and inside a Railway container req.url is the internal origin (localhost:8080), not the public host. It cannot be caught locally - on localhost:3000 the internal and public origins are the same string, so the bug is invisible until deployed behind a proxy. THE FIX belongs to this task when it is built: either derive the origin from the x-forwarded-proto and x-forwarded-host headers Railway sets, or return a RELATIVE Location header (a bare path is valid per RFC 7231 and needs no origin at all), which is the simpler and host-portable choice and matches the 'stay host-portable' rule in CLAUDE.md. WHY IT MATTERS MORE THAN IT LOOKS: /t/[code] is a fan-facing surface reached by scanning a QR code in a stadium, so the failure mode is a dead link on printed material that cannot be recalled. Check the same pattern anywhere else an absolute URL is derived from req.url before launch; /r/[token] was checked on 2026-09-21 and does NOT have it. Still Blocked on P5-BE-05 - this note records the defect so whoever builds the route does not reintroduce it, it does not unblock the row. |

| 160 | P8-INT-02 | 8 | Integration, Hardening & Launch | INT | Code | Loop prevention via origin + hash | Stop the loop where your write triggers Zoho's webhook which triggers your write again. Outbound: a row Zoho wrote last with an unchanged hash is dropped as an echo; inbound: our own write coming back is dropped. Plus: a push asserts Stage only if OUR row changed since the last sync — a redelivered job cannot revert sales' stage (found by the settle test). Conflicts on both sides go to AuditLog as sync.conflict with SoR winner. Proven by tests/zoho-sync.test.ts ('left to run, push → notify → apply settles') and live: inbound of our own sandbox deal = echo. | 2026-09-24: Done at the user's instruction — acceptance confirmed met. |

G-04 chose Resend; P3-INT-01 built the send interface against it. Nothing in the 345-task programme actually creates the account or verifies a sending domain, which is the same gap P0-OPS-06 was raised for on 2026-09-18 — a real-world prerequisite the Depends On column cannot express. | Ready | 1 |  |  | rcfworks |  | P0-PMO-03, P0-OPS-06 | Resend account exists under a BTG-controlled address; sponsorx.net verified as a sending domain with its DKIM and SPF records in Cloudflare; an API key issued and stored in the Railway variable set; a test send lands in an inbox rather than spam | G-04, Addendum A1, Guide §10 | RAISED 2026-09-21 while finishing P3-INT-01. The send interface, the queue, the idempotency ledger and the templates are all built and tested, and NOT ONE MESSAGE CAN LEAVE until this is done - an unverified sending domain means every send bounces. WHY IT IS NOT ALREADY ON THE BOARD: P0-PMO-03 only chose the vendor (a Document task) and P3-INT-01 only built the code (a Code task). Neither opens an account. This is the second time a vendor prerequisite has been invisible to the Depends On column, after the domain itself - worth remembering that 'Where: Vendor console' rows are the ones most likely to be missing entirely rather than merely blocked. THREE STEPS. (1) Create the Resend account on a BTG-controlled address, not a personal one - same reasoning as the Cloudflare Account API token: it must survive a person leaving. (2) Add sponsorx.net as a sending domain; Resend issues DKIM and SPF records to add in Cloudflare DNS, exactly as Clerk did, and they must be DNS-only rather than proxied. (3) Issue an API key and put it in Railway as RESEND_API_KEY, with EMAIL_FROM set to an address on the verified domain. WATCH THE FREE TIER: Resend's free plan is limited per day and per month, and Phase 1's volume is small enough to fit - but a bulk operation such as notifying the whole network at once would hit it, and the failure mode is a queued job retrying rather than a lost message, which is the right behaviour but looks like a stuck queue. Deliverability, not just delivery: the acceptance asks for a test send that lands in an INBOX. A verified domain that reaches spam is indistinguishable from a broken one to an athlete waiting to hear whether they were approved. 2026-09-21 THREE OF FOUR DONE. Resend account created under the icarrefound address; sponsorx.net verified as a sending domain via Cloudflare Domain Connect (CNAME send, CNAME rsend, TXT resend._domainkey - all three confirmed resolving on 1.1.1.1 and 8.8.8.8); a SENDING-ACCESS-ONLY API key issued, scoped to the domain, and set on the staging backend service. A real message was accepted by Resend (id 01a0c37c-5245-72ff-8d49-9b8300c608cf). OUTSTANDING: nobody has confirmed whether it arrived in an INBOX or in spam, which is half the acceptance - a verified domain that lands in spam is indistinguishable from a broken one to an athlete waiting to hear whether they were approved. DMARC is still absent; Resend lists it as optional and did not add it. If the test landed in spam, add _dmarc TXT 'v=DMARC1; p=none;' first. GOTCHA WORTH KEEPING: EMAIL_FROM must be QUOTED in .env. Bare, the angle brackets in SponsorX <noreply@sponsorx.net> are shell redirection when the file is sourced, so the variable comes out empty and the send fails with a misleading 'Request body must be valid JSON'. |
| 16.6 | P0-PMO-14 | 0 | Foundation & Decisions | PMO | Document | Decide how SponsorX NEXT integrates — module, not a second application | DECISION · SponsorX NEXT is a student-run high-school sports media programme: student media teams produce and SELL a sports magazine, with SponsorX owning the platform, templates, training, sales structure, publishing and the sponsor marketplace. This settles whether it goes into the product or beside it. ANSWER: into it, in the BTG tenant, sharing Sponsor, Property, the engagement funnel and asset storage — a separate app or tenant would put the magazine's QR and the athlete profile it opens on opposite sides of a trust boundary and duplicate every local business as two Sponsor rows with two Zoho sync paths. But it is NOT an extension of the Phase 1 loop: Phase 1's spine is brief-match-invite-order-deliver-report, NEXT's is recruit-assign-sell-produce-clear-publish-settle. They share the reporting end and the funnel, not the middle. It is a second product on one platform. | Done | 1 | 2026-09-22 | 2026-09-22 | rcfworks |  | P0-PMO-13, P2-BE-02 | Spec names what exists, what is new, the five touches to built code and seven open gates with owners; every line marked DECIDED or OPEN; the build is sequenced into a gated Block C rather than into Phase 1; version 1.0's errors are listed rather than quietly dropped | Blueprint §5 §7 §11 §16 §18 §21 §26 §37 §39; documentation/SponsorX-NEXT-Integration-Spec.md; Roadmap Block C | RAISED 2026-09-21. Spec at documentation/SponsorX-NEXT-Integration-Spec.md, now VERSION 2.0 — written against the concept's V3 revision and SUPERSEDING 1.0 IN FULL. Code review, not Done: nobody has reviewed it. WHY 1.0 WAS REPLACED: it read the flagship package as containing four SX-02 NIL jobs performed by paid athletes. The concept note says the social posts are STUDENT-created, twice. Everything that followed from that misreading is void — there is no CampaignOrder in a NEXT sale (an order requires an athlete and a job), so P3-BE-12's margin floor has nothing to evaluate and NEEDS NO AMENDMENT, which 1.0 wrongly claimed it did. 1.0 also said 'roughly 70% already exists'; the real figure is 35-40% by build effort, because it counted 'a school is a Property' as equal in weight to 'build a dated inventory ledger'. WHAT V3 SETTLED, all now in the spec: FEATURED is not REPRESENTED (an athlete photographed for the magazine gets a read-only profile and must CLAIM it — athlete asserts, school roster + advisor verify, guardian authorises — so nobody is silently enrolled into commercial representation); consent splits three ways (FEATURE / PROFILE / COMMERCIAL, which Agreement.kind already accommodates as data); rights are a publication gate; content conflicts and sponsor conflicts are separate workflows with separate owners, and a student loses no sales credit when SponsorX rejects a prospect for an exclusivity they could not have known about; QR_SCAN and LINK_CLICK stay distinct; BTG content needs its own licence and defaults to EDITORIAL USE ONLY; the V1 digital edition is FREE and sponsor-funded. THAT LAST ONE REMOVES THE PHASE 2 BLOCKER: the original model had parents buying 500 copies at $10 — a third of revenue in consumer card payments the Phase 1 payment policy forbids. Free digital deletes reader checkout entirely and makes the funnel bigger, since no paywall stands between a reader and an athlete profile. FOUR ENGINEERING DECISIONS TAKEN IN THE SPEC (§6), not in the concept work: (1) ONE rights ledger, not three — V3 wanted BTG kept separate from student rights and athlete consent; the capture differs but the record must not, because a publication gate is only as reliable as the single place it checks. (2) AgreementAcceptance gains typed subjects and a nullable userId — TODAY THERE IS NOWHERE TO RECORD FEATURE CONSENT, because a featured athlete has no login and a guardian has no account, and userId is required pointing at a User that requires a Clerk identity. This surfaces the moment anyone implements the claim flow. (3) Attribution is an immutable ledger row; Sponsor.assignedStudentId is the separate mutable representation — students own attribution, never the customer relationship. (4) Edition engagement is its own event stream, NOT an extension of RewardEventType: a fan's reward journey and an article engagement are different subjects, and the schema already carries a scar from collapsing two subjects into one table. FIVE TOUCHES TO BUILT CODE AND NO MORE: AthleteState gains FEATURED; AgreementAcceptance gains typed subjects; Agreement.kind extends by three values (data, not schema); Sponsor gains three columns; Role gains STUDENT and ADVISOR. P3-BE-12, Earning, RewardEventType and SponsorPackage are all untouched. SEVEN OPEN GATES with owners in spec §14, three of them real blockers: THE SCHOOL ROSTER DOES NOT EXIST (Athlete.school is free text) and claim verification runs on roster match — and a school handing a private company a list of its minors is its own data-sharing question; COMMISSION OR POINTS (V3 says a departed student's credit 'remains payable', which is money to a minor, the thing earlier drafts moved away from) — blocks payout design only, the accrual model holds either way; GPA ON A PUBLIC PROFILE must be an explicit decision confirmed against school rules, never a field added because a template has one. SEQUENCING IS THE POINT OF THIS TASK: do not build it yet. Stage 0 runs the first edition on existing primitives with zero new models, with the funnel live from day one — every featured athlete a real profile, every advertiser a real Sponsor. THE PILOT'S ONE REAL MEASUREMENT IS 20 ADVERTISERS PER SCHOOL PER EDITION: 80 sales per school per year, 240 across three schools. Every other number in the model follows from it, and if the true figure is eight the model does not degrade, it fails. That belongs in the success criteria, not the assumptions. Verified against the acceptance 2026-09-22: §3 what exists, §4 what is new, §13 the five touches to built code (5 numbered), §14 seven open gates each naming an owner (7/7), and every claim marked DECIDED or OPEN. Spec is at v2.0; v1.0's errors are listed in §0 so nobody builds from a remembered version. |

| 193 | P9-BE-10 | 9 | SponsorX NEXT | BE | Code | ContentRight - one rights ledger, three population paths | The publication gate asks one question - may we publish this asset, in this format, on this date? - and must answer it in ONE query. Student consent, athlete/guardian consent and a negotiated BTG licence populate the same table via grantorKind; three separate rights systems would mean the gate reconciles three shapes of answer per asset, which is exactly where uncleared material slips through. BTG content defaults mayReuseCommercially=false: editorial use is not a licence to resell journalism inside a sponsor's campaign. | Done | 4 | 2026-09-25 | 2026-09-25 | rcfworks | 1 | P9-BE-02 | Every asset in an edition carries a ContentRight covering the intended use; print and digital are separate permissions so a digital-first edition clears while print is outstanding; a BTG asset cannot enter a sponsor campaign without an explicit commercial grant | Spec v2.0 §5.3, §6.1 | Stage 9 Batch C (gate lifted for the build 2026-09-25). tests/next-rights.test.ts (13 tests, mutation-checked). EditionAsset (an edition's content item — not CreativeAsset, which is an athlete deliverable's) + ContentRight: ONE ledger, three population paths via grantorKind (student / athlete / guardian consent → acceptanceId; BTG / third-party licence → licenseRef; Postgres CHECKs: exactly one basis, matching the grantor). The gate is rightsGap(): ONE query (asserted by counting calls) for assets with no right covering the format on the date. rightsCleared is now COMPUTED at IN_PRODUCTION / PUBLISHED_DIGITAL (digital) and PRINTED checks print — no longer typed in (conditions input is strict; rightsCleared → 400). Tests: production refused until the last asset has a right; digital edition published while print outstanding, PRINTED refused then allowed after a print grant; BTG right defaults mayReuseCommercially=false and POST /edition-assets/{id}/campaign refuses (409) until an explicit commercial grant; consent from someone else doesn't cover an asset. | 2026-09-25: Done at the user's instruction — acceptance met; tests green in CI. |
| 194 | P9-BE-11 | 9 | SponsorX NEXT | BE | Code | FEATURED athletes, three-part consent and the claim flow | The correction that makes NEXT safe: being featured is not being represented. AthleteState gains FEATURED - created by editorial, never applied for, read-only, invisible to matching, holds no rates. Claiming needs three assertions: athlete initiates, school roster plus advisor verifies, guardian authorises. AgreementAcceptance must gain typed subjects with a nullable userId FIRST - today a featured athlete has no login and a guardian has no account, so there is nowhere to record feature consent at all. Agreement.kind extends by FEATURE, PROFILE and COMMERCIAL as data. | Done | 4 | 2026-09-25 | 2026-09-25 | rcfworks | 1 | P9-BE-04 | A featured athlete has a public profile and can receive no invitation; consent is recorded for a subject with no login; commercial activation is impossible without guardian authorisation; GPA is absent unless separately decided | Spec v2.0 §5.4, §6.2 | Stage 9 Batch C (gate lifted for the build 2026-09-25). tests/next-rights.test.ts (13 tests, mutation-checked). AthleteState FEATURED (edge FEATURED → UNDER_REVIEW only). POST /featured-athletes (editorial; no email, no consent); GET /public/athletes/{slug} (exact keys — no legal name, contact, age, GPA). A featured athlete: invitation refused, rate refused (new guard), absent from matching (tested). AgreementAcceptance gains TYPED SUBJECTS (userId nullable + athleteId/studentId; Postgres CHECK exactly one) — POST /consents records consent with no login; a minor's only via their verified guardian. Agreement.kind + FEATURE/PROFILE/COMMERCIAL (data). Claim: public POST .../claim ('that's me'; roster result never returned) → school verifies (roster match required + that school's ADVISOR; other school 403) → UNDER_REVIEW → guardian linked + verified → approve → ACTIVE refused until the guardian's COMMERCIAL consent is recorded, then allowed. Roster is SIMULATED (RosterEntry, seeded for the pilot; real roster = spec §14 gate 1). P0-LEG-01 tracked on the Legal sheet, not gating. GPA: no column anywhere (asserted). | 2026-09-25: Done at the user's instruction — acceptance met; tests green in CI. |

EditionEvent + EngagementType (QR_SCAN print; LINK_CLICK/PROFILE_VIEW/CAMPAIGN_VIEW/CTA_CLICK digital) — its own stream, RewardEventType untouched (asserted from the schema). PUBLIC POST /public/editions/{id}/events, rate-limited, only on a published edition and a real target. Sponsor report gains adPlacements + editionEngagement {print, digital}, never pooled; ad spend now counts in media value. | 196 | P9-BE-13 | 9 | SponsorX NEXT | BE | Code | Sponsor ownership, immutable attribution, and the two conflict checks | Students own attribution, never the customer relationship. A SalesAttribution row is never updated and never deleted - including when the student graduates, is suspended, or the sponsor churns - because a student's portfolio depends on it. Sponsor gains ownership (SCHOOL | SPONSORX | STUDENT_ORIGINATED), schoolPropertyId and assignedStudentId, the last being current representation and not ownership. Separately the two conflict checks are distinct workflows with distinct owners: a Content Conflict holds content for edit; a Sponsor Acceptance rejection carries a reason code, notifies the student and COSTS THEM NO SALES CREDIT. | Done | 3 | 2026-09-25 | 2026-09-25 | rcfworks | 1 | P9-BE-07 | Graduating a student changes no attribution row; reassigning an account touches only assignedStudentId; a rejected prospect leaves the student's standing intact and offers a redirect where a category is open | Spec v2.0 §5.1, §5.6, §6.3 | Stage 9 Batch B (gate lifted for the build 2026-09-25). tests/next-students.test.ts (21 tests, HTTP through real auth/scope, mutation-checked). SalesAttribution is immutable IN POSTGRES (trigger sales_attribution_immutable; deletion only via a deliberate SET LOCAL purge for teardown/lawful erasure). Sponsor gains ownership (default SPONSORX) / schoolPropertyId / assignedStudentId; a student's first sale to a new account sets STUDENT_ORIGINATED, never moved after. Tests: graduating (INACTIVE) leaves attribution rows byte-identical and staff can still read them; direct UPDATE/DELETE refused; POST /sponsors/{id}/assigned-student changes only assignedStudentId (every other column snapshot-compared). Sponsor Acceptance Check: POST /prospects/{id}/decision — reject needs a reason code (422 without), queues notify.email student.prospectDeclined to the student (or guardian), leaves attribution + points identical, and on CATEGORY_EXCLUSIVE offers open categories (excludes the held presenting-sponsor category and minor-unsuitable ones). Content Conflict Check is the rights workflow — Batch C. | 2026-09-25: Done at the user's instruction — acceptance met; tests green in CI. |

GET/POST /commission-rules, POST /commission-rules/{id}/revise (BTG_ADMIN, FINANCE). Proven: the highest-priority matching rule in effect applies (kind rule over global, sponsor rule over both, a future-dated rule only once its window opens); a revision is version n+1 from now with version n's window closed, and the earlier moment still resolves to n. Postgres refuses any other change to a rule row. 'Never alters a closed payout': rules are read in exactly one place — resolveRates at contract time (static guard pins it) — and the result is frozen and booked; proven that a revision leaves every snapshot, every ledger entry (full-row fingerprint) and the property dashboard identical, while the next order uses the new version. Payouts (2S5-BE-04/05) draw on those entries and never ask the rules. Rates are SIMULATED (ledger design §5). Tests: tests/phase2-ledger.test.ts, mutation-checked. |

## Sheet: Legend
| Legend |  |
| --- | --- |
| How to read every phase sheet. |  |
| THE DAILY RULE |  |
| During the day | Tag your task In progress in THIS file. Set Owner and Date Started. Move to Code review, then Done, as it advances. |
| At end of day | Whatever state your tasks are in — finished, half-done, blocked, untouched — update the Google Sheet to match. That is the copy other people read. |
| In commits | Put the task ID (e.g. 2S5-BE-04) in the branch name and commit message. |
| If a task is wrong | Fix its definition in the Markdown plan files, by pull request. Never quietly reinterpret it here. |
| WHERE THE WORK HAPPENS |  |
| Document | A written decision or specification. No system is touched — the output is a document people agree on. |
| Zoho dashboard | Configuration clicked through the Zoho CRM admin UI (Setup → Modules and Fields, API credentials, users). |
| Vendor console | Configuration in a vendor's own UI — Railway, Cloudflare, Clerk, the payment or email provider. |
| Code | Work in the repository: schema, domain logic, routes, worker jobs, UI, tests, CI. |
| Code + console | Both: something configured at the vendor AND wired in the codebase. |
| Design tool | Visual design work producing artwork, diagrams or screen designs. |
| Document + code | A written policy that must then be enforced in the codebase. |
| External · counsel | Performed by a lawyer, not the team. Longest lead times in the programme — start these early. |
| COLUMNS YOU EDIT |  |
| Shaded columns | Status · Weight · Date Started · Date Done · Owner · Notes. Everything else is the plan of record. |
| CATEGORIES |  |
| FE | Frontend Engineering — Next.js routes, server components, client islands, forms, charts, responsive and theme work |
| BE | Backend Engineering — Prisma schema and migrations, domain functions, route handlers, worker jobs, queue, scoping |
| INT | Integrations — Zoho CRM + Books, Clerk, R2, transactional email, social-platform APIs, webhooks |
| OPS | DevOps & Infrastructure — Railway project and services, environments, CI/CD, migrations, backups, monitoring, runbooks |
| ART | Design & Art — Brand system, theme tokens, logo and asset production, screen design, illustration, QR and report design, marketing visuals |
| QA | Quality Assurance & Testing — Unit, authorisation matrix, E2E, accessibility, performance, UAT scripts |
| SEC | Security & Compliance (engineering) — Tenant isolation, field-level authz, consent capture, PII handling, audit logging, MFA |
| DATA | Data & Analytics — Metric provenance, rollups, Content Value Score, seed and import jobs, reporting datasets |
| LEG | Legal & Policy — Counsel-approved templates, guardian authorisation, privacy review, reward terms, payment policy. Not developer work — but it blocks developer work. |
| PMO | Product, Docs & Delivery — Gate decisions, §38 deliverables, RBAC matrix, ERD, Zoho field mapping, admin guide, handoff documentation |
| SOURCES |  |
| Phase 1 | Master Development Blueprint v2.0 (§) + .claude/stack-decision.md Addendum A |
| Phases 2–4 | Phases 2–4 Detailed Developer Specifications v1.0, August 2026 (Spec §) + Blueprint §32–34 |
